empty:hidden mb-14" data-page-title="Track todos" data-page-href="/en/agent-sdk/todo-tracking" id="content"><span data-as="p">Claude Code provides the <a href="/docs/en/tools-reference#task-tool-availability" class="link">task-tracking tools</a> by default only on the models listed under <a href="#model-availability" class="link">Model availability</a>. Newer models track multi-step work without a written todo list, so on those you don’t need anything on this page for Claude to work through multi-step tasks.</span>
<span data-as="p">In a session that has the task-tracking tools, Claude keeps a written todo list, updating each item’s status as it works. You see each change in the message stream as a structured tool call. Opt a session in only when your application reads those tool calls, whether to log task activity or to render its own progress display.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="model-availability" aria-labelledby="_R_59deehkd74t9_"><a href="#model-availability" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Model availability"></a><span id="_R_59deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Model availability</span></h2>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">The following tools are available by default only on Claude Opus 4.7, Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Haiku 4.5, and earlier models. On every other model, including model IDs Claude Code doesn’t recognize, they aren’t available unless you opt in:</span><ul>
<li><code>TodoWrite</code></li>
<li><code>TaskCreate</code></li>
<li><code>TaskGet</code></li>
<li><code>TaskUpdate</code></li>
<li><code>TaskList</code></li>
</ul><span data-as="p">Wherever the tools are available, Claude Code provides the four Task tools, or <code>TodoWrite</code> instead when you set <code>CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TASKS=0</code>.</span><span data-as="p">
This default set applies in Claude Code v2.1.268 and later, which the TypeScript Agent SDK bundles from v0.3.268.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">On a model that doesn’t have the tools by default, unless you opt a session in, you see no <code>tool_use</code> blocks for them in the message stream. The Agent SDK applies these defaults through the Claude Code binary that it bundles. If you point <code>pathToClaudeCodeExecutable</code> (TypeScript) or <code>cli_path</code> (Python) at your own Claude Code install, you get whichever tools that install provides, under its own defaults. To see the exact set in a running session, <a href="/docs/en/tools-reference#check-which-tools-are-available" class="link">check which tools are available</a>. To opt a session in, do one of the following:</span>
<ul>
<li>Name one of the tools in the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions#allow-and-deny-rules" class="link"><code>allowedTools</code></a> (TypeScript) or <code>allowed_tools</code> (Python) option</li>
<li>List the tools in the <code>tools</code> option, which restricts the session’s built-in tools to the ones it names. Include the tools you want alongside the other built-in tools you use</li>
<li>Set <code>CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1</code> in the <code>env</code> option, as the examples on this page do. In TypeScript, <code>env</code> replaces the subprocess environment, so spread <code>...process.env</code> to keep inherited variables. In Python, <code>env</code> is merged on top of the inherited environment</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="todo-lifecycle" aria-labelledby="_R_d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#todo-lifecycle" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Todo lifecycle"></a><span id="_R_d9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Todo lifecycle</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Claude moves each todo through a predictable lifecycle:</span>
<ol>
<li><strong>Created</strong>: Claude adds the todo as <code>pending</code> when it identifies a task</li>
<li><strong>Activated</strong>: Claude sets the todo to <code>in_progress</code> when it starts the work</li>
<li><strong>Completed</strong>: Claude marks it completed when the task finishes successfully</li>
<li><strong>Removed</strong>: Claude deletes a todo it no longer needs by setting <code>status: &quot;deleted&quot;</code> in a <code>TaskUpdate</code> call</li>
</ol>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="when-claude-creates-todos" aria-labelledby="_R_j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#when-claude-creates-todos" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: When Claude creates todos"></a><span id="_R_j9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">When Claude creates todos</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">In a <a href="#model-availability" class="link">session that has the task-tracking tools</a>, Claude creates todos for most multi-step work, such as:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Complex multi-step tasks</strong> requiring three or more distinct actions</li>
<li><strong>User-provided task lists</strong> when multiple items are mentioned</li>
<li><strong>Longer operations</strong> that benefit from progress tracking</li>
<li><strong>Explicit requests</strong> when users ask for todo organization</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Claude may skip todos for very short or single-step requests.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="examples" aria-labelledby="_R_r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#examples" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Examples"></a><span id="_R_r9deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Examples</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Before running these examples, install the Claude Agent SDK by following the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/quickstart" class="link">quickstart</a>. Every example on this page shares the same permission setup and exit behavior:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Permission mode</strong>: the example prompts ask Claude to do real work on a project, so each example sets <code>permissionMode: &quot;acceptEdits&quot;</code> (TypeScript) or <code>permission_mode=&quot;acceptEdits&quot;</code> (Python) to auto-approve the file edits that work produces. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions#permission-modes" class="link">Permission modes</a> for the alternatives.</li>
<li><strong>Turn limit</strong>: each example runs until the agent finishes and yields its final result message. If a session reaches its turn limit first, that result message has the <code>error_max_turns</code> subtype. Check <code>subtype</code> to detect that ending.</li>
<li><strong>Error handling</strong>: these examples use single-shot <code>query()</code> calls. After yielding an <code>error_max_turns</code> result, <code>query()</code> raises an error that includes <code>Reached maximum number of turns</code>. Each example wraps its loop in a try block to exit cleanly when that happens. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/agent-loop#handle-the-result" class="link">Handle the result</a> for the result subtypes.</li>
</ul>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">The task system messages, <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdktasknotificationmessage" class="link"><code>SDKTaskNotificationMessage</code></a> (TypeScript) or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#tasknotificationmessage" class="link"><code>TaskNotificationMessage</code></a> (Python) among them, report background tasks such as backgrounded commands and subagents. In the message stream, you see todo activity as <code>tool_use</code> blocks in the assistant messages.</span></div></div>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="monitor-todo-changes" aria-labelledby="_R_139deehkd74t9_"><a href="#monitor-todo-changes" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Monitor todo changes"></a><span id="_R_139deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Monitor todo changes</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The following example watches the assistant stream for <code>TaskCreate</code> and <code>TaskUpdate</code> <code>tool_use</code> blocks and prints a <code>+</code> line with each new task’s subject and an update line with each status change’s task ID and new status. Use this shape when you want a log of task activity rather than a rendered display. The <code>+</code> lines don’t include the assigned IDs, so this log can’t match updates back to their creates. To keep that correlation, capture the IDs as <a href="#display-progress-in-real-time" class="link">Display progress in real time</a> does.</span>
<span data-as="p">The streamed <code>tool_use</code> input is the raw shape the model emitted. Claude Code repairs some close-but-incorrect key names before execution, mapping <code>id</code> or <code>task_id</code> to <code>taskId</code> and <code>active_form</code> to <code>activeForm</code>, but that repair is not reflected in the stream. Read <code>TaskUpdate</code> input fields defensively, as both examples on this page do, rather than assuming the canonical name is always present.</span>

<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="display-progress-in-real-time" aria-labelledby="_R_1b9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#display-progress-in-real-time" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Display progress in real time"></a><span id="_R_1b9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Display progress in real time</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The following example watches the assistant stream for <code>TaskCreate</code> and <code>TaskUpdate</code> <code>tool_use</code> blocks and keeps a map of tasks keyed by task ID in a <code>TaskTracker</code> class, rerendering a progress summary on every change. The summary counts completed and in-progress tasks and shows each active item’s <code>activeForm</code> label in place of its <code>subject</code>. Use this shape when your application maintains a progress display instead of logging each event.</span>
<span data-as="p">The assigned task ID isn’t in the <code>TaskCreate</code> input. Claude Code delivers each tool’s structured output on the user message that carries its <code>tool_result</code> block, in the <code>tool_use_result</code> field. For <code>TaskCreate</code>, that object is documented for TypeScript as <code>TaskCreateOutput</code> under <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#tool-output-types" class="link">Tool Output Types</a>, and in Python the field is a plain dict of the same shape. The tracker pairs each <code>tool_result</code> block with its <code>tool_use</code> call by <code>tool_use_id</code> and reads <code>task.id</code> from the paired message’s <code>tool_use_result</code>. Claude can read the list back with <code>TaskList</code> and one task’s full details with <code>TaskGet</code>.</span>
